❯ The specs, ADRs, plans and tasks are the source; where this prompt and a plan 
  differ, the plan wins, and tell me.                                           
  Implement the local runtime of specs/008-deployment following its plan and    
  tasks. Sections 1.1 to 1.6 of spec 008 are the source for services, ports,    
  start order, demo data and configuration; where this prompt and the spec      
  differ, the spec wins.                                                        
  - Dockerfile: multi-stage on node:24-alpine pinned by version and digest. A   
  build stage (npm ci, build) that is also the tools image; a runtime stage     
  with production dependencies only, a non-root user, the HEALTHCHECK of        
  section 1.6 (dist/healthcheck.js against /health/live; the load balancer      
  checks readiness), and an exec-form entrypoint that runs node directly        
  (DEP-R20).                                                                    
  - compose.yaml exactly as table 1.1: postgres, redis, a one-shot migrate      
  service, api-1 and api-2 sharing one YAML anchor with their own REPLICA_ID    
  logged on every line and stop_grace_period: 40s, nginx, and tools in the      
  compose profile "tools". Every image pinned by digest, every port published   
  on 127.0.0.1 only, METRICS_PORT never published. The replicas start only      
  after migrate exits with code 0.                                              
  - No .env needed: literal demo values per section 1.4, including the demo     
  JWT_SECRET and CURSOR_SECRET of DEP-R35, which the service refuses when       
  NODE_ENV is production.                                                       
  - nginx per section 1.5 and spec 007: round robin over both replicas with     
  upstream keep-alive; proxy_next_upstream as DEP-R15; limit_req per IP with    
  the defaults of spec 007, 429 with Retry-After; client_max_body_size 32k;     
  413, 429 and its own 502, 503 and 504 as problem details; X-Forwarded-For     
  replaced with the client address (SEC-R19); X-Request-Id propagation; proxy   
  and keep-alive timeouts per spec 007; no upstream on METRICS_PORT. The        
  replicas get TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS set to the compose network, so only nginx is 
  trusted for X-Forwarded-For.                                                  
  - scripts/seed.ts and npm run seed exactly as section 1.2: the four users of  
  table 1.2 with their fixed ids, accounts and deposits (2500.00 EUR and        
  1000.00 USD, 500.00 EUR, 150000 JPY), created through the API at              
  http://nginx:8080, and changing nothing when run again (DEP-R10 to DEP-R12).  
  Settlement accounts come from the migrations.                                 
  - Makefile with targets: up, down, logs, seed, token, test, reconcile, all    
  running through docker compose (seed, token and reconcile with docker compose 
  run --rm tools). The e2e and load targets come in phase 11.                   
  - README quickstart with the exact commands; it must work with only Docker    
  installed.                                                                    
    Verify from a clean state and show me the output: docker compose down -v,   
  docker compose up --build -d, wait until healthy, make seed, mint a token     
  with make token, make a transfer through nginx with curl, and show that       
  requests reach both replicas. Tick only the 10-runtime tasks; e2e ACs are     
  ticked in phase 11.

      1 +// node dist/healthcheck.js: the container healthcheck of the im
        +age (DEP-R21, section 1.6 of spec                               
      2 +// 008). Liveness, not readiness, so a database outage never mak
        +es Docker or ECS replace every                                  
      3 +// container; the load balancer checks readiness.               
      4 +import { checkLiveness } from './platform/health/liveness-probe.
        +js';                                                            
      5 +                                                                
      6 +process.exitCode = await checkLiveness({ port: process.env['PORT
        +'] });                                                          
  ⎿  Created src/platform/health/liveness-probe.ts (+31 -0)
       1 +/** How long the probe waits for an answer, below the image's `
         +HEALTHCHECK --timeout=3s`. */                                  
       2 +export const LIVENESS_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS = 2000;                 
       3 +                                                               
       4 +/** The service's default `PORT` (section 1.2 of spec 007). */ 
       5 +const DEFAULT_PORT = 3000;                                     
       6 +                                                               
       7 +export interface LivenessProbeOptions {                        
       8 +  /** `PORT` as the environment has it; the default 3000 when u
         +nset. */                                                       
       9 +  port: string | undefined;                                    
      10 +  timeoutMs?: number;                                          
      11 +}                                                              
      12 +                                                               
      13 +/**                                                            
      14 + * The container healthcheck (DEP-R21): requests `http://127.0.
         +0.1:${PORT}/health/live` with Node's                           
      15 + * `fetch`, with no shell or extra binary in the image, and ret
         +urns 0 when it answers 200 and 1                               
      16 + * otherwise: another status, no answer within the timeout, a r
         +efused connection or an invalid                                
      17 + * `PORT`. It writes nothing, so Docker records only the exit c
         +ode.                                                           
      18 + */                                                            
      19 +export async function checkLiveness(options: LivenessProbeOptio
         +ns): Promise<0 | 1> {                                          
      20 +  const port = options.port === undefined ? DEFAULT_PORT : Numb
         +er(options.port);                                              
      21 +  if (!Number.isInteger(port) || port < 1 || port > 65535) retu
         +rn 1;                                                          
      22 +  try {                                                        
      23 +    const response = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${String(por
         +t)}/health/live`, {                                            
      24 +      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(options.timeoutMs ?? LIVENESS
         +_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS),                                            
      25 +    });                                                        
      26 +    await response.body?.cancel();                             
      27 +    return response.status === 200 ? 0 : 1;                    
      28 +  } catch {                                                    
      29 +    return 1;                                                  
      30 +  }                                                            
      31 +}

        4 +interface Instruction {                                       
        5 +  keyword: string;                                            
        6 +  args: string;                                               
        7 +}                                                             
        8 +                                                              
        9 +interface Stage {                                             
       10 +  name: string | undefined;                                   
       11 +  from: string;                                               
       12 +  instructions: Instruction[];                                
       13 +}                                                             
       14 +                                                              
       15 +/** The Dockerfile's instructions by stage, continuation lines
          + joined and comments dropped. */                              
       16 +function stagesOf(text: string): Stage[] {                    
       17 +  const lines = text                                          
       18 +    .replace(/\\\n/g, ' ')                                    
       19 +    .split('\n')                                              
       20 +    .map((line) => line.trim())                               
       21 +    .filter((line) => line !== '' && !line.startsWith('#'));  
       22 +  const stages: Stage[] = [];                                 
       23 +  for (const line of lines) {                                 
       24 +    const [keyword = '', ...rest] = line.split(/\s+/);        
       25 +    const args = rest.join(' ');                              
       26 +    if (keyword.toUpperCase() === 'FROM') {                   
       27 +      const match = /^(?:--platform=\S+\s+)?(\S+)(?:\s+AS\s+(\
          +S+))?$/i.exec(args);                                          
       28 +      if (match === null) throw new Error(`unexpected FROM ${a
          +rgs}`);                                                       
       29 +      stages.push({ from: match[1] ?? '', name: match[2], inst
          +ructions: [] });                                              
       30 +    } else {                                                  
       31 +      const stage = stages.at(-1);                            
       32 +      if (stage === undefined) throw new Error(`${keyword} bef
          +ore any FROM`);                                               
       33 +      stage.instructions.push({ keyword: keyword.toUpperCase()
          +, args });                                                    
       34 +    }                                                         
       35 +  }                                                           
       36 +  return stages;                                              
       37 +}                                                             
       38 +

          +9a-f]{64}$/;

  ⎿  # SuperCool Finances

     Balance service for SuperCool Finances: customer accounts, a double-en

       6  
       7  ## Quick start
       8  
       9 +### Run it with Docker only                                    
      10 +                                                               
      11 +Prerequisite: Docker Engine 24 or later with Compose v2.20 or l
         +ater. No Node, no `.env` file: `compose.yaml` holds visibly fak
         +e demo values, which the service refuses when `NODE_ENV` is `pr
         +oduction`.                                                     
      12 +                                                               
      13 +```sh                                                          
      14 +# Build the image and start Postgres, Redis, the migrations, tw
         +o replicas and nginx;                                          
      15 +# returns once every service is healthy. The API is on http://l
         +ocalhost:8080 (Swagger UI at /docs).                           
      16 +docker compose up --build --wait                               
      17 +                                                               
      18 +# Create the demo users' accounts and deposits through the API,
         + and print them as JSON.                                       
      19 +# Running it again changes nothing.                            
      20 +docker compose run --rm tools npm run --silent seed            
      21 +                                                               
      22 +# Mint a token for demo-customer-1 and list their accounts (250
         +0.00 EUR and 1000.00 USD).                                     
      23 +TOKEN=$(docker compose run --rm tools npm run --silent token --
         + --sub 0192f0a0-0000-7000-8000-00000000d0c1 --role customer)   
      24 +curl -s http://localhost:8080/v1/accounts -H "Authorization: Be
         +arer $TOKEN"                                                   
      25 +                                                               
      26 +# Transfer 10.50 EUR from one of their accounts to another acco
         +unt in EUR: use the ids printed by the seed.                   
      27 +curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8080/v1/accounts/<EUR account 
         +id>/transfers \                                                
      28 +  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: applicati
         +on/json' \                                                     
      29 +  -H 'Idempotency-Key: my-first-transfer' \                    
      30 +  -d '{"destinationAccountId": "<destination account id>", "amo
         +unt": "1050", "currency": "EUR"}'                              
      31 +                                                               
      32 +# Check every balance against the ledger, then stop the stack (
         +add -v to delete the database).                                
      33 +docker compose run --rm tools npm run --silent reconcile       
      34 +docker compose down                                            
      35 +```                                                            
      36 +                                                               
      37 +The demo users are `demo-operator` (`0192f0a0-0000-7000-8000-00
         +000000d0f1`, role `operator`) and `demo-customer-1` to `demo-cu
         +stomer-3` (`0192f0a0-0000-7000-8000-00000000d0c1` to `...d0c3`,
         + role `customer`); a token is valid for 15 minutes. nginx balan
         +ces requests round robin over `api-1` and `api-2`, and every lo
         +g line of a replica carries its `replicaId`, so `docker compose
         + logs api-1 api-2` shows which replica served each `X-Request-I
         +d`. Only `127.0.0.1` is published: nginx on 8080, the replicas 
         +on 3001 and 3002, Postgres on 55432 and Redis on 6379.         
      38 +                                                               
      39 +With `make`, the same commands are `make up`, `make seed`, `mak
         +e token` (or `make token SUB=<uuid> ROLE=operator`), `make reco
         +ncile`, `make logs` and `make down`; `make test` runs the typec
         +heck, lint, format check and unit tests in the tools image.    
      40 +                                                               
      41 +### Develop on the host                                        
      42 +                                                               
      43  Prerequisites: Node 24 (the version in `.nvmrc`, for example wi
          th `nvm use`) and Docker.

⏺ Phase 10-runtime is built and verified from a clean state. Every 10-runtime
  task in specs 007 and 008 is ticked. Nothing is committed yet; that's for
  /ship.

  What changed
  - Image: a two-stage Dockerfile on node:24.21.0-alpine, pinned by digest. The
    build stage is also the tools image. The runtime stage has production
    dependencies and dist/ only, runs as node with node dist/main.js as process
    1, and has the dist/healthcheck.js healthcheck on /health/live.
  - compose.yaml: table 1.1 with api-1 and api-2 sharing one anchor
    (stop_grace_period: 40s). The replicas start only after migrate exits 0.
    Every image is pinned by digest, every port is on 127.0.0.1, and
    METRICS_PORT is never published. The literal demo values need no .env,
    TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS is the network's fixed subnet, and tools is in the tools
    profile.
  - nginx: the per-IP limit, the 32k body limit, the timeouts, the request id
    and X-Forwarded-For replacement of spec 007, plus round robin with
    re-resolving and the retry rule of DEP-R15. Its own 413, 429, 502, 503 and
    504 are problem details.
  - Service: replicaId is on every log line and in no response, and the config
    refuses the demo JWT_SECRET and CURSOR_SECRET when NODE_ENV is production.
    src/platform/db/migrate.ts is now the single migration path, used by npm run
    migrate:up, the migrate service and the test helpers.
  - Seed, Makefile, docs: scripts/seed.ts and npm run seed, the Makefile
    targets, the README quick start for Docker only, and the AGENTS.md commands
    and repository map.
  - Spec change, with your approval: DEP-R36 and DEP-AC25 now also allow the
    OpenAPI example Idempotency-Key. The approval is recorded in plan 000 §11,
    and spec 008 stays Approved.

  What was verified (docker compose down -v, then up --build -d, then the steps
  you listed)
  - Start: all services healthy, and migrate exited 0 after applying all 8
    migrations. docker compose up --build --wait also exits 0.
  - make seed: demo-customer-1 got EUR 250000 and USD 100000, demo-customer-2
    EUR 50000, demo-customer-3 JPY 150000. A second run returned the same
    account ids and created nothing (still 4 accounts, 5 transactions, 5 audit
    records).
  - make token: prints one 297-character line.
  - Transfer with curl through nginx: 201 with Location. The same
    Idempotency-Key replayed with idempotent-replayed: true, and the balances
    went to 248950 and 51050 EUR.
  - Both replicas: 20 reads with ids rr-1 to rr-20 alternated strictly. api-1
    served the odd ones, api-2 the even ones. The transfer ran on api-1 and its
    replay was answered by api-2. No response mentions a replica.
  - nginx's own answers: a body over 32 KB got the 413 problem, /metrics
    answered 404, and with both replicas stopped the 502 problem came back with
    Retry-After: 1 and requestId "gw-1".
  - Gates: npm run check passes (350 tests). npm run test:integration passes
    (329). npm run trace -- --require unit,integration passes. make test passes
    inside the tools image, and make reconcile is clean.

  Where I went beyond or differ from the plan
  - Not in the plan: the Makefile isn't in it at all, and the README sections
    were scheduled for 12-infra. I did both because you asked.
  - Extra file: I added docker/nginx/nginx.conf, because worker_processes 1
    can't go in the template.
  - make test: it runs only npm run check. The integration suite has to run on
    the host, because the DEP-AC25 test runs gitleaks with Docker.
  - gitleaks test: it copies files into the container instead of mounting them,
    because colima shares only the home directory.

  What's left or worth knowing
  - Migrate noise: every docker compose run tools re-runs migrate through the
    dependency chain of table 1.1. It applies nothing, and the status lines go
    to stderr.
  - Trusted proxy caveat: requests from the host reach the containers from the
    gateway 10.210.0.1, which is inside the trusted subnet. A client calling a
    replica directly on 127.0.0.1:3001 could therefore set its own
    X-Forwarded-For. This is local only; nginx always replaces the header.
  - Dependabot: spec 008 §1.6 says Dependabot updates the image pins, but no
    task or config covers it.
  - Flaky test: one scratch-database test failed once and passed on every later
    run, including the full suite. I couldn't find a cause.
  - Phase 11: the e2e ACs and the make e2e and make load targets.

⏺ All four changes are in, and npm run check, npm run test:integration and npm 
  run trace all pass on the host (351 unit tests, 329 integration tests).

  1. Only nginx is trusted for X-Forwarded-For. nginx now has the fixed address
  10.210.0.10, and the replicas trust only 10.210.0.10/32. Other containers get
  addresses from 10.210.0.128/25 only, so none can take nginx's. A new test next
  to the SEC-R19 one in test/unit/deployment/nginx.test.ts checks the address
  and runs the app's trust-proxy setting with the value from compose.yaml. On
  the live stack, a call straight to 127.0.0.1:3001 with X-Forwarded-For: 
  6.6.6.6 logged the real peer, 10.210.0.128, not 6.6.6.6. Through nginx, the
  forged header was replaced with the client's own address.

  2. make test runs the whole suite with only Docker. The tools image now
  includes gitleaks 8.30.1, checked against its official sha256 for each
  architecture. The DEP-AC25 test uses that binary when it's installed, checks
  that its version matches the CI job, and otherwise falls back to the pinned
  Docker image (which is what happens on the host). From a clean stack (docker 
  compose down -v, then make test), it exited 0:
  - npm run check: 351 tests passed.
  - npm run test:integration against the stack's Postgres and Redis: 329 tests
    passed.
  - npm run trace -- --require unit,integration: passed.

  3. Dependabot. .github/dependabot.yml covers docker, docker-compose, npm and
  github-actions, weekly. I added docker-compose because Dependabot's docker
  updater doesn't read compose.yaml. The task is ticked under 10-runtime in
  specs/008-deployment/tasks.md.

  4. The scratch-database test. The one that failed once is "creates a database,
  migrates it to the latest migration and drops it afterwards" in
  test/integration/support/scratch-database.test.ts. Its file passed 10 times in
  a row, so I found no cause and changed nothing. The single failure didn't
  record its error message.

  Beyond the four items, I made one small docs fix: the README and AGENTS.md
  lines about make test said it only ran the unit check, so I updated both to
  describe the full suite.

  Needs your decision: DEP-AC25's Given in spec 008, and the note in plan 008
  §7, both say gitleaks runs "from its Docker image pinned by digest". The test
  now prefers the binary from the tools image, so the spec wording no longer
  matches. Changing it is a spec change, so I left it alone. Should I amend the
  Given and the plan note to "the tools image's binary, or the pinned Docker
  image when there is none"?

✻ Churned for 15s · done 10:19 PM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  Two Medium findings and five Low. The first Medium is a security issue, so per the rules this phase should not ship yet. There are no commits on phase/10-runtime yet, so the review covers the uncommitted and untracked changes. Nothing was run: no tests, no npm run trace, no Docker.

     1. Medium (security): production accepts the committed demo cursor secret as JWT_SECRET, and the reverse
     - Where: src/platform/config/config.ts:273
     - What is wrong: refuseDemoSecrets only compares each variable with its own demo value (env[variable] === demo). DEP-R07 says to refuse when JWT_SECRET or CURSOR_SECRET "equals one of the demo values committed in compose.yaml".
     - How to trigger: start with NODE_ENV=production, JWT_SECRET=demo-only-cursor-secret-for-docker-compose-00000 and any other 48-byte CURSOR_SECRET. That value passes the 32-byte length rule and the "different from JWT_SECRET" rule, so the service starts.
     - Impact: the token signing key is a value published in the repository. Anyone can mint operator tokens and make deposits or reversals.
     - Fix: refuse each variable when it equals any value of DEMO_SECRETS. Add the swapped pair to test/unit/deployment/demo-secrets.test.ts.
     - AC: DEP-AC05 / DEP-R07. The AC only tests matching pairs, so this case is untested.

     2. Medium (failure handling): nginx's other errors are HTML, not problem details
     - Where: docker/nginx/templates/default.conf.template:61-65
     - What is wrong: error_page maps only 413, 429, 502, 503 and 504. nginx's own 400 (bad request, or "Request Header Or Cookie Too Large"), 408 and 414 use its default HTML page, as text/html, with no X-Request-Id and no requestId.
     - How to trigger: curl -H "X-Pad: <9000 bytes>" http://localhost:8080/v1/accounts, or a request URI over 8 KB.
     - Impact: this breaks SYS-R24 ("every error" is application/problem+json) at the stack's only public entry point. Clients that parse problem details fail.
     - Fix: add error_page 400 408 414 494 … to named locations, built the same way as the existing ones (for example a /problems/bad-request type). Extend nginx.test.ts to cover them.
     - AC: SYS-R24. Missing AC: no AC covers nginx's own 4xx answers.

     3. Low: the hard-coded network subnet can block up and the planned e2e stack
     - Where: compose.yaml:169 (and :31, :127)
     - What is wrong: the subnet 10.210.0.0/24 is fixed, and postgres and redis now join that network too.
     - How to trigger:
       - Any existing Docker network, VPN or LAN on 10.210.0.0/24 makes docker compose up (including npm run infra:up) fail with "Pool overlaps".
       - After docker compose stop the network stays while the ports are freed. The 11-e2e harness (COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=scf-e2e) then passes its port check and fails creating a second network with the same subnet.
     - Impact: DEP-R01 ("one command") fails on some hosts, with a confusing error.
     - Fix: make the subnet a tunable (${SCF_SUBNET:-…}, with nginx's address derived from it), or document the constraint. Have the e2e harness check for the network as well as the ports.
     - AC: DEP-R01 / DEP-AC01.

     4. Low: build tooling with no spec, plan or task behind it
     - Where: Makefile:1-47, Dockerfile:7-20
     - What is wrong: No spec or plan lists the Makefile; it appears only in AGENTS.md and the README. Its test target is also why the build stage downloads gitleaks from GitHub. Because the runtime stage depends on the build stage, every image build now needs GitHub access, and fails on any TARGETARCH other than amd64 or arm64.
     - How to trigger: run docker compose up --build without access to github.com, or build for another architecture.
     - Impact: an extra network dependency and failure point in building the production image. It also bypasses the "no behaviour without an AC" rule.
     - Fix: add the Makefile and the gitleaks step to plan 008 (and tasks) with the owner's approval. Alternatively, move gitleaks into a separate tools stage that runtime does not depend on.
     - AC: missing AC (DEP-R09 covers the tools service only in general terms).

     5. Low (docs drift): the plans say the replicas trust the whole subnet; the code trusts only nginx
     - Where: specs/008-deployment/plan.md:35 and specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:132, against compose.yaml:31
     - What is wrong: both plans say TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS is the network's subnet. The code uses nginx's fixed /32 with an ip_range reservation. The code is the stricter and better choice. Plan 008's file table also omits src/platform/health/liveness-probe.ts, Makefile and .github/dependabot.yml.
     - How to trigger: read the plans next to compose.yaml.
     - Impact: the next phase may "fix" the code back to the subnet, which would let any container on the network spoof X-Forwarded-For.
     - Fix: update both plans and the file table.
     - AC: SEC-AC10 (plan text only).

     6. Low (tests): the gitleaks allowlist check doesn't enforce "only regular expressions"
     - Where: test/integration/deployment/gitleaks.test.ts:200-211
     - What is wrong: the test checks the TOML file with text regexes. It rejects paths, commits, disabledRules and [[rules]], but not stopwords, the legacy [allowlist] table, or regexTarget/condition changes.
     - How to trigger: add stopwords = ["demo"] to .gitleaks.toml. The test still passes, and gitleaks then ignores any finding containing "demo".
     - Impact: DEP-AC25's "only regular expressions" can be silently weakened.
     - Fix: parse the file as TOML and allow only the keys description, regexTarget = "secret" and regexes in each [[allowlists]] entry.
     - AC: DEP-AC25.

     7. Low (failure handling): the migrate command hides why a connection failed
     - Where: src/platform/db/migrate.ts:96-99
     - What is wrong: an error that isn't a database error is reported by name only, for example "migrate: the migrations failed (Error)". The code (ECONNREFUSED, ENOTFOUND, ETIMEDOUT) is safe to print and is dropped.
     - How to trigger: point MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL at a stopped database or a host name that doesn't resolve.
     - Impact: a failed AWS migration task or migrate container gives the operator nothing to act on.
     - Fix: also print error.code when it matches ^E[A-Z]+$, never the message.
     - AC: DEP-R03 / DEP-R05 (operability; not asserted by any AC).

     Deferred, already planned: the e2e ACs DEP-AC01–04, 06, 08–11 and 13, including whether up --wait handles the one-shot migrate, the replica-loss behaviour and the round-robin counts (11-e2e); the Terraform ACs DEP-AC14–24 and 26 (12-infra); migrations through RDS Proxy and advisory-lock pinning (ADR-0019, 12-infra); SEC-AC01 and SEC-AC05 behaviour through nginx (11-e2e).

     - Money: no findings. Amounts stay digit strings in the seed and bigint in its fake API.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings. No domain or persistence code changed.
     - Idempotency: no findings. The seed's fixed per-user keys and re-run rules match DEP-R11.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

❯ The audit says fix before shipping. Fix all seven:                            
  1. In production, refuse JWT_SECRET and CURSOR_SECRET when either equals any  
  of the demo values committed in compose.yaml, not only its own (DEP-R07); add 
  the swapped pairs to test/unit/deployment/demo-secrets.test.ts.               
  2. nginx answers its own 400, 408, 414 and 494 as 400                         
  /problems/malformed-request problem details with its request id, built like   
  the existing named locations, the same rule the service applies to requests   
  its HTTP parser refuses; extend nginx.test.ts and check one oversized header  
  and one URI over 8 KB on the live stack.                                      
  3. Make the network configurable with a default, so no .env is needed: one    
  variable such as SCF_SUBNET_PREFIX defaulting to 10.210.0, from which the     
  subnet, nginx's fixed address, the ip_range and TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS are       
  derived. Document the overlap error and the variable in the README. Add a     
  note to the 11-e2e tasks of spec 008 that the e2e stack uses its own prefix   
  and checks that its network is free, not only its ports.                      
  4. Move gitleaks to a separate tools stage built from the build stage, which  
  the runtime stage does not depend on, and point the tools service at it, so   
  building the production image never needs GitHub. As the owner I approve the  
  Makefile and that tools stage: add both to plan 008's file table and a ticked 
  10-runtime task, and record my approval in plan 000 section 11.               
  5. Update plans 007 and 008 to the /32 trust of nginx's fixed address with    
  the ip_range reservation, and add src/platform/health/liveness-probe.ts, the  
  Makefile and .github/dependabot.yml to plan 008's file table.                 
  6. Make the .gitleaks.toml check an allowlist of lines, not a denylist: only  
  comments, blank lines, [extend], useDefault = true, [[allowlists]],           
  description, regexTarget = "secret" and regexes are accepted, so stopwords, a 
  legacy [allowlist], paths or any other key fails the test. Add a test case    
  with stopwords.                                                               
  7. When migrate fails on a non-database error, also print error.code when it  
  matches ^E[A-Z]+$, never the message.                                         
     Change nothing else. Run npm run check, npm run test:integration, npm run  
  trace and make test from a clean stack.

⏺ All seven audit findings are fixed. npm run check (352 tests), npm run 
  test:integration (331) and npm run trace pass on the host, and make test
  passes from a clean stack (down -v first), running all three in the tools
  service.

  1. Demo secrets swapped (DEP-R07): in production, JWT_SECRET and CURSOR_SECRET
     are now refused when either equals either demo value. The DEP-AC05 test
     covers both swapped pairs and both swapped at once.
  2. nginx's own parser errors: a 400, 408, 414 or 494 from nginx is now
     answered as a 400 /problems/malformed-request with its request id, and =400
     makes the status line 400 too.
     - What the live check caught: the first version sent a URI over 8 KB to a
       500 HTML page. A request line nginx can't parse has no URI, so it can't
       be sent to a named location. The fix uses an internal location,
       /_nginx/malformed-request.
     - Side effect, also fixed: a direct request to that internal path got
       nginx's HTML 404. I mapped nginx's own 404 to the service's
       /problems/not-found body; the service's own 404s still pass through
       unchanged.
     - Live results: a 9000-byte header → 400 problem; a URI over 8 KB → 400
       problem; the internal path → 404 problem; /no-such-path → the service's
       404 problem.
  3. Configurable network: SCF_SUBNET_PREFIX (default 10.210.0) sets the subnet,
     the reserved address range, nginx's address and the trusted /32 together;
     I checked this with SCF_SUBNET_PREFIX=10.99.7. The README gives Docker's
     exact error (invalid pool request: Pool overlaps with other one on this 
     address space) and the fix. The 11-e2e stack.ts task in spec 008 now says
     the e2e stack uses its own prefix and checks that its subnet is free, not
     only its ports.
  4. gitleaks in its own stage: the Dockerfile now has build, tools and runtime
     stages, and the tools service builds the tools stage. Building the
     production image ran only build and runtime steps, and its log never
     mentions gitleaks.
     - Why the tools stage starts from node: it starts from the pinned node
       image and copies /app from the build stage, rather than FROM build,
       because DEP-AC12 requires every FROM to be pinned by digest.
     - Recorded: your approval of the Makefile and the tools stage is in plan
       000 §11, both are in plan 008's file table, and there's a ticked
       10-runtime task.
  5. Plans: plans 007 and 008 now describe the /32 trust of nginx's fixed
     address and the reserved range. Plan 008's file table adds
     liveness-probe.ts, the Makefile and dependabot.yml.
  6. gitleaks allowlist check: it now accepts only comments, blank lines,
     [extend], useDefault = true, [[allowlists]], description, regexTarget = 
     "secret" and single-literal regexes. A new DEP-AC25 test confirms that
     stopwords, a legacy [allowlist], paths, commits, disabled rules,
     conditions, rules, unanchored or catch-all regexes, and a regex line with
     two values all fail.
  7. migrate errors: a non-database failure now prints its system code, for
     example migrate: the migrations failed (Error, ECONNREFUSED), and never the
     message, URL or host. A test covers it.

  Beyond the seven items, I updated the AGENTS.md wording to "build, tools and
  runtime stages" and "the image's tools stage", to match the code.

  One wording mismatch left for you: spec 008 §1.1, §1.4 and DEP-R09 still call
  the tools service "the build stage" of the image. It's now the tools stage,
  which copies the build stage and adds gitleaks. That wording is spec text, so
  I didn't change it. Should I amend it?

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  Nothing is committed on phase/10-runtime yet, so this audit covers the uncommitted and untracked changes against main. I found five Low findings and nothing that blocks shipping. The phase touches no domain, ledger or idempotency code.

     Findings

     1. Low: the seed can wait up to about 70 s for readiness instead of 60 s
     - Where: scripts/seed.ts:155-170, with REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000 at line 18.
     - What is wrong: waitUntilReady checks the 60 s deadline only after each /health/ready request returns. Each request can take up to 10 s before it times out.
     - How to trigger it: nginx is up but the replicas accept connections and never answer. Each poll then hangs 10 s, so the seed gives up after about 70 s.
     - Impact: DEP-R12 says "within 60 seconds". The unit test can't see this because its fake HTTP client answers at once.
     - Fix: limit each poll's timeout to the time left before the deadline, or pass a timeout through SeedRequest.
     - AC: DEP-R12 (DEP-AC07).

     2. Low: the DEP-AC25 allowlist check accepts regexes that allow far more than one value
     - Where: test/integration/deployment/gitleaks.test.ts:166-174 (allowlistProblems).
     - What is wrong: a regex passes if it starts with ^, ends with $, and matches exactly one value of the allowed list.
     - How to trigger it: add regexes = ['''^(5b0d7f1e-6c2a-4e8b-9f3d-2a1c4e6b8d0f|AKIA[A-Z2-7]{16})$'''] to .gitleaks.toml. It matches only one allowed value, so the test passes, but it allowlists every AWS access key id.
     - Impact: the guard for DEP-R36 ("anchored to the whole value") can be bypassed by alternation or wildcards. The current file is correct; only the regression check is weak.
     - Fix: require each regex to equal '^' + escapeRegExp(value) + '$' for exactly one allowed value. Add a negative case like the one above to the second test.
     - AC: DEP-R36 / DEP-AC25.

     3. Low: the migrate CLI hides the reason for failures that are not database errors, and a comment overstates the lock
     - Where: src/platform/db/migrate.ts:24-26 and :108-114.
     - What is wrong: node-pg-migrate's lock runs in fail mode (it uses pg_try_advisory_lock). A second concurrent run therefore throws "Another migration is already running", and a checkOrder violation throws a plain Error too. The CLI prints only migrate: the migrations failed (Error). The comment's "two runs at once apply each migration once" reads as if the second run waits; it fails.
     - How to trigger it: start docker compose run --rm migrate while another migration run (the stack's migrate service, or later the AWS task) is in progress.
     - Impact: the run fails with exit 1 and no actionable message. That is correct but slow to diagnose, especially in the AWS pipeline (DEP-R28).
     - Fix: whitelist the known node-pg-migrate messages (lock held, order check), which contain no credentials, or map them to fixed texts. Reword the comment to say a concurrent run fails.
     - AC: missing AC (failure handling, DEP-R04/DEP-R28 context).

     4. Low: Dependabot will propose major bumps that break the specified stack versions
     - Where: .github/dependabot.yml:6-24.
     - What is wrong: there are no ignore rules for version-update:semver-major, so node:25/26, postgres:17/18 and redis:8 will be offered. The nginx bump will go to the 1.29 mainline, while plan 008 asks for a stable release.
     - How to trigger it: the first weekly run after a new major tag.
     - Impact: merging one would contradict AGENTS §4 (Node 24, PostgreSQL 16, Redis 7), spec 008 table 1.1, and the RDS PostgreSQL 16 of DEP-R29. Today it is a review risk, not a defect.
     - Fix: add ignore: [{dependency-name: "*", update-types: ["version-update:semver-major"]}] to the docker and docker-compose entries, plus a rule that keeps nginx on 1.28.x.
     - AC: DEP-R22 (section 1.6).

     5. Low: the README's Docker-only flow can run a stale tools image
     - Where: README.md:16-20.
     - What is wrong: docker compose up --build --wait doesn't rebuild tools, because its profile isn't active. The next docker compose run --rm tools npm run seed (or token, reconcile, migrate:up) reuses the old tools image, whose sources predate the change. make up avoids this with --profile tools build; the README commands don't.
     - How to trigger it: run the README flow once, change scripts/seed.ts or add a migration, then run the README commands again.
     - Impact: scripts from old sources run against a freshly built API.
     - Fix: document docker compose --profile tools up --build --wait, or docker compose run --rm --build tools ....
     - AC: missing AC (docs drift; DEP-R09 context).

     Deferred, already planned: DEP-AC01, AC02, AC03, AC04, AC06, AC08, AC09, AC10, AC11 and AC13 plus the e2e harness and retrying client (11-e2e); SEC-AC10, SEC-AC05 and SEC-AC01 e2e (11-e2e); DEP-AC14 to AC24 and AC26, the Terraform, docs/deployment/aws.md, the deploy runbook and npm run infra:validate in CI (12-infra).

     Categories without findings

     - Spec conformance: no other findings. Every ticked task's AC has a test whose name carries its ID: DEP-AC05, AC07, AC12, AC25 and SEC-AC26, AC29, AC33, AC36.
     - Money: no findings. The seed keeps amounts as digit strings, and the fake API in its test uses bigint.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings. No transaction, locking or retry code changed.
     - Idempotency: no findings. The seed's keys are valid visible ASCII and fixed for each user and step. Its "history empty" rule keeps a rerun from moving money after the keys expire.
     - Security: no findings. The demo secrets are refused in production by name, never by value; tokens are never printed; ports are published on 127.0.0.1 only; .env is kept out of the image; the replicas trust only nginx's /32. Finding 2 is the only weak spot.
     - Failure handling: no further findings beyond 1 and 3. The timeouts are ordered (2 s locks < 5 s statement_timeout < 25 s request timeout < 30 s nginx), stop_grace_period 40 s is above the process's 37 s hard limit, and nginx's 502/503/504 are problem details.
     - Tests: no further findings beyond 2. The tests use no sleeps or timing assumptions.
     - Docs drift: no further findings beyond 5.

     Follow-ups: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5
     Verdict: ready to ship

     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/test/integration/deployment/gitleaks.test.ts

  The five fixes
  1. Seed readiness: each readiness poll is now limited to the time left before
     the 60 s deadline. A new test whose fake client never answers stops at
     exactly 60 s, with every poll's timeout within the time left.
  2. gitleaks allowlist check: each regex must now be exactly ^ + one escaped
     allowed value + $. The test now refuses your alternation example and a .
     wildcard variant.
  3. migrate: a run started while another holds the lock, and a failed order
     check, now print fixed messages naming the cause, each with a test. The
     comment now says a concurrent run fails at once rather than waiting.
  4. Dependabot: major updates are ignored for docker and docker-compose, and
     nginx is held below 1.29.
  5. Fresh tools image: the README and the Makefile now run docker compose build
     --quiet tools before every tools command.
     - I didn't use run --build: it put 149 lines of build progress on stdout,
       which breaks TOKEN=$(...).
     - I didn't use run --quiet-build either: I couldn't confirm which Compose
       version added it (release notes, compose build reference), and the README
       promises Compose v2.20.
     - I updated the matching AGENTS.md row too.

  About the e1 failure (no code changed during the test, as you asked):
  - What happened: request mt-e-1 took 60.002 s and got a 504. nginx logged
    "upstream timed out … while connecting to upstream" for api-1's old address,
    10.210.0.131, and never tried api-2.
  - Partial cause: 60 s is nginx's default proxy_connect_timeout, which the
    template doesn't set. A stopped container's address drops new connections
    instead of refusing them, so nginx waits the full timeout.
  - Still unexplained: nginx had already been logging that api-1 couldn't be
    resolved, yet it still used the old address. And proxy_next_upstream error 
    timeout with tries 2 should have retried on api-2.
  - Suggestion: set a short proxy_connect_timeout, a couple of seconds, and find
    out why the retry didn't happen. DEP-AC11 in phase 11 exercises exactly
    this case.

✻ Sautéed for 1m 53s · done 11:03 PM

❯ Step e is a real defect; fix it before closing the phase.                     
  1. Find out why nginx did not retry on api-2 after the connect timeout to     
  api-1, although proxy_next_upstream error timeout and                         
  proxy_next_upstream_tries 2 are set. Reproduce it with nginx's error log at   
  info level and tell me the cause (for example how the resolve parameter       
  treats a name that no longer resolves, or how the tries are counted), then    
  fix it so a request whose first replica cannot be reached is passed to the    
  other one, as DEP-R15 allows.                                                 
  2. Set proxy_connect_timeout to 2s, with a comment: inside the compose        
  network a connection is established in milliseconds, and a stopped container  
  drops packets instead of refusing them. As the owner I approve adding that    
  value to the timeouts of section 1.7 of spec 007; record my approval in plan  
  000 section 11 and keep spec 007 Approved. Extend nginx.test.ts to check it.  
  3. Rerun step e of the manual test exactly as before, with the same 5 reads   
  and 1 deposit after docker compose stop api-1, and append its raw output to   
  reports/manual-test-10.log. Every request must succeed, and none may take     
  more than about 2 s.                                                          
     Change nothing else. Run npm run check, npm run test:integration and npm   
  run trace.

⏺ Confirmed: with resolve, retries only occur if re-resolving changes the peer
  set during a failed attempt—A retried and succeeded, B saw a resolve change so
  no retry (504), and C (fixed addresses) retried and succeeded. I'll now check
  nginx's source code to pin down the exact rule.

       x_http_upstream_round_robin.c)

⏺ Confirmed in the source: when a resolve refresh changes upstream peers
  mid-retry (e.g. removing api-1), nginx's config check fails and returns the
  original 504 instead of retrying — made nearly certain here by the 60s connect
  timeout versus ~5s refresh interval. Since item 2 doesn't depend on fixing
  this, I'm implementing it now: setting proxy_connect_timeout 2s, updating spec
  007 §1.7, recording it in plan 000, and adding the test.

      115  ### 1.7 Load balancer
      116  
      117  - **Body size.** nginx's `client_max_body_size` is 32k, so a b
           ody up to 32 KB reaches the service, which answers a body abov
           e 16384 bytes with the problem details of SEC-R10. A body abov
           e 32 KB gets nginx's own 413, rendered as problem details with
            type `/problems/payload-too-large` by an `error_page 413` loc
           ation, as for 429.
      118 -- **Timeouts.** nginx `proxy_read_timeout` and `proxy_send_tim
          -eout` are 30 s and its upstream `keepalive_timeout` is 60 s; i
          -n AWS the ALB idle timeout is 60 s. The service's keep-alive t
          -imeout is 65 s, longer than both, so the service never closes 
          -a connection that the load balancer is about to reuse, which w
          -ould show up as random 502s (SEC-R34).                        
      118 +- **Timeouts.** nginx `proxy_connect_timeout` is 2 s: inside t
          +he compose network a connection is established in milliseconds
          +, and a stopped container drops packets instead of refusing th
          +em, so a longer wait would only delay passing the request to t
          +he other replica (DEP-R15). nginx `proxy_read_timeout` and `pr
          +oxy_send_timeout` are 30 s and its upstream `keepalive_timeout
          +` is 60 s; in AWS the ALB idle timeout is 60 s. The service's

          +ever closes a connection that the load balancer is about to re

⏺ Item 2 is complete — proxy_connect_timeout 2s added with comment, spec/plan

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Cause found: with `resolve`, a re-resolve that changes the upstream's 
     peers during a failed connect makes nginx skip the retry. nginx only 
     retries if the peer set is unchanged since the request started 
     (`rrp->config != *peers->config` → busy), so it returns the first attempt's
     504. The 2 s connect timeout shrinks that window but doesn't close it. How
     should the retry be made reliable? → Fixed replica addresses (Recommended)

  SCF_SUBNET_PREFIX, outside the ip_range, and remove resolve from the upstream
  listing. First, let me search for where resolve or the resolver is referenced.

         +addresses, `server ${API_1_ADDRESS}:3000` and `server ${API_2_A
         +DDRESS}:3000`, without `resolve`. nginx passes a failed request
         + to the other server only if the upstream's servers have not ch
         +anged since the request started (`ngx_http_upstream_get_round_r
         +obin_peer` answers busy when they have), and `resolve` changes 
         +them when a stopped replica's name stops resolving, which lost 
         +the retry in the phase-10 manual test; with fixed addresses the
         + servers never change, and a replica that restarts keeps its ad

         +t_timeout 2s` gives up on a replica that is gone, whose address

         +ection 1.7 of spec 007). `worker_processes 1` keeps the round r
         +obin strictly alternating, which DEP-AC09's count of at least 5

⏺ The step-e defect is fixed, and the rerun passes: with api-1 stopped, all 5
  reads and the deposit succeeded, none taking longer than 2.01 s. npm run check
  (353 tests), npm run test:integration (333) and npm run trace pass.

  Why nginx didn't retry. I reproduced it with a throwaway nginx on the stack's
  network, using the same template with its error log at info level. The resolve
  parameter was the cause:
  - Timeline: the request went to api-1's address, which was still current when
    it started. While nginx waited the default 60 s connect timeout, its
    periodic re-resolve found api-1 gone ("could not be resolved (3: Host not
    found)") and removed that server from the upstream.
  - Why that stops the retry: nginx only passes a request to another server if
    the upstream's servers haven't changed since the request started. In
    ngx_http_upstream_get_round_robin_peer, if (peers->config && rrp->config != 
    *peers->config) goto busy; finds no peer, so the client gets the first
    attempt's 504.
  - Proof, three variants against a stopped api-1:

  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
  │                      Variant                      │       Result        │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ resolve, 2 s connect timeout, no re-resolve       │ retried; 200 in 2.0 │
  │ during the wait                                   │  s                  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ resolve, 8 s connect timeout, one re-resolve      │ no retry; 504 after │
  │ during the wait                                   │  8 s                │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ fixed addresses, 8 s connect timeout              │ retried; 200 after  │
  │                                                   │ 8 s                 │
  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘

  The fix (fixed addresses, as you chose)
  - Replica addresses: api-1 is .11 and api-2 is .12, both derived from
    SCF_SUBNET_PREFIX and outside the dynamic ip_range, like nginx's .10.
  - Upstream: it lists those addresses with no resolve and no resolver, so its
    servers never change and the retry always reaches the other replica. A
    restarted replica keeps its address, so nginx finds it again.
  - Tests and docs: the DEP-R15 test checks this, and plan 008, its ticked task
    line and the README describe it.

  The 2 s connect timeout. proxy_connect_timeout 2s is in the template with the
  comment you described, and nginx.test.ts checks it. Spec 007 §1.7 has the new
  value; plan 000 §11 records your approval, and spec 007 stays Approved.

  Rerun of step e (appended to reports/manual-test-10.log):
  - Setup: the stack was recreated with nginx at .10, api-1 at .11 and api-2 at
    .12, then api-1 stopped.
  - Reads: 200 in 0.03 s, 200 in 2.01 s, then 0.005, 0.004 and 0.003 s. The 2.01
    s read went to .11, timed out, and was passed to .12 (nginx logged "504,
    200").
  - Deposit: 201 in 0.02 s, and N1 went from 10600 to 10700, exactly once.
  - After docker compose start api-1: api-1 came back at .11. It got traffic
    again after nginx's 10 s fail_timeout; the reads straight after the restart
    all went to api-2.

  The deposit used a new key, mt-deposit-4, because reusing mt-deposit-3 would
  only replay the earlier deposit. Every request also printed its time_total.
  Those are the only differences from the original step e.

  The log has no token-shaped strings, and no repro containers are left. The
  repro's config files are still in ~/.scf-nginx-repro, because the delete was
  refused; you can remove them with ! rm -rf ~/.scf-nginx-repro.
